Comments
1. MAIN   FINANCE  
                 
       Bank book maintained without opening balance and closing balance(osp-267)
      
                                                                 The bank book for the year 2011-12 has been maintained without opening balance as on 1.4 2011 and closing balance as
on 31.3.2012.Simply the receipt  and expenditure have been reflected in the bank book. Audit asked the local authority through objection memo to supply the
opening balance and closing balance as on 31.3.2012 but no reply was furnished to audit.A copy of balance sheet prepared by Agasti & associates,a charted
accountant firm engaged by BDA was supplied to audit .Basing on the balance sheet of that firm for the year 2010-11 and 2011-12, the opening balance and
closing balance has been worked out.The local authority is requested to supply the opening balance and closing balance of bank book for the year 2011-12 to audit
.
                                                                     The details of head wise receipt and expenditure has been furnished in para 18.4 of the AR.
The details of closing balance as on 31.3.2012 as per Audit /Bank book:
Cash in hand                                          :   Rs. nil
In bank                                                     :   Rs.73689087.16
Total as per audit                                    :   Rs.73689087.16
C.B as per cash book/Bank book         :  Rs.73689087.16
Difference                                                 :    Rs.nil
 
 
 
 
2. DIVISION-I :
 Details of CB on 31.3.12 as per Audit/as per cash book :
a)Cash in hand :         NIL
b) in Bank         :Rs.7852821.93
 
 
Irregular maintenance of Cash Book
The Cash Book contains neither OB  on 1.4.11 nor CB on 31.3.12 . Hence both are worked out by taking the bank positions from receipt  expenditure abstract
register . The details of bank position has been given in Cash Book neither at the end of transaction of each day nor at the end of each month and on end of each
financial year . Also the Cash Book is not maintained by working out CB at the end of transaction of each day . This process of maintenance of Cash Book is quite
irregular . Hence, the local authority should take into account the above fact for future guidance. The details of head wise receipt and expenditure figure of Div-I
have been furnished in Para 18.2 of this AR.
 
       
 
 
3. DIVISION-II :
 
 8 / 204

[page 9]
 AUDIT REPORT 
09-06-2016
        Details of CB on 31.3.12 as per Audit/as per cash book :
                          In cash    :                   69.00
                          In bank    : 2,30,68,288.34  
                                        --------------------------------
                              TOTAL  :  2,30,68,357.34    
        
Irregular maintenance of Cash Book
The Cash Book contains neither OB  on 1.4.11 nor CB on 31.3.12 . Hence both are worked out by taking the bank positions from receipt  expenditure abstract
register . The details of bank position has been given in Cash Book neither at the end of transaction of each day nor at the end of each month and on end of each
financial year . Also the Cash Book is not maintained by working out CB at the end of transaction of each day . This process of maintenance of Cash Book is quite
irregular . Hence the local authority should take into account the above fact for future guidance  . 
 
                                                       The details of head wise receipt and expenditure figure of Div-II  have been furnished in Para 18.1 of this AR
 
4. DIVISION-III  :
           1. Reconciliation of differential  amount between closing balance of Cash Book and audit figure :
                  C.B as per audit                    : Rs.10154487.72
                   C.B. as per cash book        : Rs.10129785.72
                    Difference                 ( less) : Rs.24702.00
                  A sum of Rs.24,702.00 in shape of cash has miscalculated in the receipt side of Cash Book due to the reasons as described in details in Para No. 11.2
 
          2.  Details of Closing Balance on 31.3.12 as per Cash book    :
                           Cash in hand                     :              290.00
                              In Bank                             :1,01,29,495.72
                                            TOTAL                : 1,01,29,785.72
 
                      
Irregular maintenance of Cash Book
The Cash Book contains neither OB  on 1.4.11 nor CB on 31.3.12 . Hence the OB is calculated by taking the OB of different bank accounts as on 1.4.11 and cash
position from Cash Book . The details of bank position has been given in Cash Book neither at the end of transaction of each day nor at the end of each month and
on end of each financial year . Also the Cash Book is not maintained by working out CB at the end of transaction of each day . This process of maintenance of Cash
Book is quite irregular . Hence, the local authority should take into account the above fact for future guidance  . 
 
                                                       The details of head wise receipt and expenditure figure of Div-III  have been furnished in Para 18.5 of this AR

Irregular maintenance of Cash Book
The Cash Book contains neither OB  on 1.4.11 nor CB on 31.3.12 . Hence both are worked out by taking the bank positions from receipt  expenditure abstract
register . The details of bank position has been given in Cash Book neither at the end of transaction of each day nor at the end of each month and on end of each
financial year . Also the Cash Book is not maintained by working out CB at the end of transaction of each day . This process of maintenance of Cash Book is quite
irregular . Hence, the local authority should take into account the above fact for future guidance  .The details of head wise receipt and expenditure figure of Div-IV
have been furnished in Para 18.3 of this AR.

Comments
Physical verification of stock and stores
It is seen from the stock register that physical verification of stock and stores is not being conducted regularly. as per Rule-111 of OGFR,a physical
verification of all stores should be made at least once in every year by the Head of Office concerned or such other officer as may be specially authorized
by him.Non  conduct of regular physical verification may lead to mis-utilisation,defalcation and damaging of perishable items causing loss. The local
authority is suggested to conduct physical verification as required under the rule and report compliance to audit.

Production of Advance ledger and outstanding advance register of Division - IV (OSP-252) :
While checking the cash book w.r.t. paid vouchers it is noticed that a total amount of Rs.148500.00 has been paid to different officials as advance for incurring
expenditure during 2011-12.The outstanding advance at the beginning of the year was Rs.93819.00. As such the total amount outstanding for adjustment comes to
Rs.242319.00. Out of this said amount Rs.155400.00 has been adjusted during the year under audit leaving the balance outstanding to Rs.86919.00. Though the
balance outstanding against the persons has been shown in the cash book, the details of payments and the period to which the outstanding amount relates are not
furnished. To verify the outstanding position, the outstanding advance register and the advance ledger maintained under rule-100 of ODA Rule-19 could not be
produced to audit for verification.

adjusted against Vr. no. 218 and 219/ dated 28.2.2013 for Rs. 12296.00 and 53120.00 respectively. Balance amount Rs. 21503.00 has been returned by Sri N.K
Sahu, Ex. A.E.(Electrical) to this office vide money receipt no 47357/date 4-3-2013

Non maintenance of Advance ledger and Advance Register: (OSP-5)
The payment ,adjustment and outstanding position of advance was worked out by audit from the cash book maintained during 2011-12. The outstanding advance
register and the advance ledger could not be made available to audit in spite of issue of objection memo. In response to objection memo the local authority replied
 as follows-
The details of payment and the period to which the outstanding amount relates are verifiable from the cash book itself from which the observation have been
reported by audit. Hence it is construed that no  advance ledger and outstanding advance register are being maintained in Division -I.Though there is a specific
provision made under Rule 100 of ODA rule 1983 and Rule 213(2) of OGFR Vol-I.
The Local authority is suggested to maintained the advance ledger and outstanding advance register so as to keep proper watch over the advance position as well
as to avoid embezzlement in future.
Non adjustment of advance relating to previous years:(OSP-5)
It is seen from the above table that a sum of Rs.135394.00 relating to the period prior to 2011-12 has not been adjusted till 31.3.2012. In response to objection
memo the Local authority replied that a sum of Rs.101658.00 outstanding against Sri. S.K.Prusty was adjusted during 2012-13 which was verified by audit.As
regards Rs.12800.00 ,it was stated that the where about of the person namely N.Nayak could not be ascertained due to which the amount is still outstanding.Since
the name of the sanctioning authority and the period to which the amount relates could not be ascertained in audit, the entire amount is considered as loss to the
institution and the amount may be recovery from Sri.N.Nayak, Ex.J.E. as per circular no.15179/DLFA dtd.28.9.2013 and 2221/F, dtd.8.3.2002 and compliance
reported to audit.
 
Non adjustment of Advance paid during 2011-12:(OSP-5)
It is seen from the above table that a sum of Rs.105252.00 relating to the period  2011-12 has not been adjusted till 31.3.2012. In response to objection memo the
Local authority replied that a sum of Rs.122594.00 outstanding against Sri. N.K.Sahoo was adjusted during 2012-13 which was verified by audit. As regard the
adjustment of RS.3594.00 the Local Authority remained silent. Hence  it is construed that the amount is  still outstanding for more than three years which is
considered as loss to the institution .Hence as per  circular no.15179/DLFA dtd.28.9.2013 and 2221/F, dtd.8.3.2002. the amount is may be recovered from Sri Uttam
Mishra ,Ex.E.E. and Sri. P.K. Mohanty, A.E and compliance reported to audit.

PARA: 11 MISAPPROPRIATION & DEFALCATION
11.1 - Excess booking of Expenditure in the Cash Book , Div - III  , OSP-162
 
 
Reference : Vr. No.80/31.6.11 , Rs.700.00 , Div - III Cash BOOK
 
                             On checking the above cited voucher, it is found that a sum of Rs.700.00 (cash) has been booked in Cash Book as
expenditure towards “digital plan Xerox” . But in the bill, it it is seen that actual bill amount is Rs. 100.00 and the amount pass for payment
made is Rs. 700.00 resulting misappropriation of Rs. 600.00 which needs recovery from the persons held responsible .
                          Through issue of audit objection memo the local authority was asked to clarify the reason for such excess booking of Rs.
600.00 in the Cash Book as well as pass for payment of the same amount . In response to audit objection memo the local authority returned
the  half margin objection memo without any compliance . Hence it is construed by audit that the local authority admitted the objection for which
the said amount of Rs. 600.00 needs recovery from the following persons who are considered responsible for such irregularity .
Responsible Person for this paragraph
Slno Name Designation Adress Amount(In Rs:)
1 Sri Yudhisthir Patalsing Ex-Cashier,Div-III BDA,Bhubaneswar 300.00
2 Sri Jaladhar Swain Ex-Executive Engineer,
Div-III
BDA, Bhubaneswar 300.00
       
11.2 - Misappropriation of cash by reducing the cash in hand position to the tune of Rs. 24702.00 ,Div-III,   ,OSP - 192 to 211
While checking the cash transaction of Div-III for the period 1.4.11 to 31.3.12 with ref. to Cash Book,  miscellaneous receipt, paid vouchers and
bank pass book, it is noticed that the closing balance of cash in hand position as on 31.3.12 , should be Rs.24,992.00 as calculated on the
basis of the transaction made in the Cash Book . But as against the above amount, Rs.290.00 has been shown as cash in hand in the Cash
Book as on 31.3.12 . As such there is deficit of Rs 24702.00 in cash in hand . Deficit of cash in hand resulted in misappropriation of cash  by
the cashier Sri Patalsingh who was the custodian of the cash during the above period  . The details of the cash position are furnished below  :
Cash in hand position as on 31.3.12 worked out on the basis of the Cash Book transaction from 1.4.11 to 31.3.12  :

from the  salary of Sri Y.Patalsing in shape of  monthly instalments and administrative action is taken which was verified by audit from the o.o. and
from  the salary aquittance of staff and found true .The monthly instalments has been stated from Dec.2014 and continuing till now and
Rs.29145.00 has realised upto Feb.16 .Hence in support of the above fact the above para is dropped .

11.4 - Misappropriation of cash by booking false advance in the Cash Book to the tune of Rs. 2,85,380.00  ,OSP-210 ,211
 
While checking the cash transaction in the Cash Book of Div-III with ref. to paid vouchers, miscellaneous receipts, bank pass books , it is
noticed that a sum of Rs. 2,85,380.00 was shown to have been paid to different persons towards advance as detailed in above  Annexture- V .
The advances are booked from the available cash & reflected in the inner column of the Cash Book in support of which no acknowledgement
receipt/hand receipt of the recipients are available . Further in support of adjustment of the advances, no vouchers and Advance Ledger are
made available to audit . Audit  through its objection memo no. 84 dated  6.01.16  asked the local authority  to produce the following records to
justify the payment of advance :
i)                    Application of the advance holder
ii)                   Sanction order of the competent authority

But nothing could be made available to audit  till date . As such audit holds the view that the advance payments are fictitiously made in the

PARA: 14 AUDIT OF EXPENDITURE
14.1 - Irregular Payment of Conveyance Allowance OSP-41,42
On checking of the pay acquittance  roll w.r.t. the bank book and vouchers, it is noticed that an amount of Rs. 4292099.00 has been paid
towards conveyance allowance to the staffs of BDA during the year 2011-12 (Details attached). As per F.D. memo no.-6181/F dtd.22.2.91 , the
conveyance allowance has been discontinued w.e.f.1.3.1990. Under section 124 of ODA Act 1982, BDA is empowered to make regulations on
the payment of salary, allowances and condition of service of the secretary,other officers and employees of the authorities. Before framing the
regulation,BDA should take prior approval of the State government. Further under section-4 of ODA Act 1982, it is envisaged that the Secretary
and other officers and employees of the authority shall be entitled to receive from the funds of the authority such salaries and allowances if any
and shall be governed by such conditions of service as may be determined by regulations made in this behalf.

conveyance allowance. Hence in absence of government approval, the payment made towards conveyance allowance is not admissible in audit .

authority did not obtain  previous approval of the state Govt.,the payment made in this regard is considered as irregular and unauthorised.As such
Rs.4292099.00 is suggested for recovery from the concerned employees in case of failure to obtain ex post facto approval from government in
finance department.

14.9 - Hiring of vehicles with low fuel efficiency  ,  OSP-109,110
 
                                                                               On checking of the paid vouchers of main finance on hiring of vehicles w.r.t . the copies of
Log books ,it is observed that the vehicles(BOLERO)  hired from Nanda Travels for exclusive use by the C.E. cum E.M. of BDA  is having fuel
efficiency of 10 kmpl. But as per Finance Department O.M.No.34085/F, BBSR dtd.29.9.12 on Hiring of private vehicles for official use by the
offices under the State Govt., the field officers are permissible to hire Bolero with minimum avg. Mileage of 12kmpl. As a result of hiring of
vehicles with low fuel efficiency, the main purpose of achieving economy as well as efficiency in the administrative expenditure through hiring of
private vehicles instead of purchasing new vehicles is highly hampered. Hence BDA incurs financial loss of Rs.11209.00 the details of which
are given below. Objection memo has been issued to know the reason for hiring of vehicles with low fuel efficiency . But in reply to the objection
memo the Local authority remained silent. Hence the entire amount of Rs.11209.00 needs to be recovered from the person responsible for
giving permission for hiring of such vehicles i.e. Sri Nihar Ranjan Mishra,A.E. Mechanical.

14.11 - Use of hired vehicles on Sundays and Public Holidays OSP-113
                On checking of the paid vouchers of main finance on hiring of vehicles w.r.t . the copies of Log books ,it is observed that  some of the
vehicles has been used in Sundays and Public Holidays. According to Finance Department Office Memorandum No.10954/F, Dtd.14.3.2001,
clause 4(vi-c), No officer can use any Govt. vehicle on Sunday and Public Holidays under the N.I. Act, unless so permitted by the Chief
Secretaries and Secretaries for Heads of Department and District officers by the Collector of the District. Hence the reason and permission for
use of the hired vehicles on Sundays and public holidays were asked to produced to audit but no reply was received from the local authority. As
such the use of vehicle by the officers as detailed below are considered as unauthorized and cost of fuel consumed needs recovery from the
concerned officers

14.12 - Irregular and unauthrised engagement of  DLR Main FinanceOSP- 118,119
                                               
                             On checking of the paid vouchers of main finance w.r.t. Pay Bill Register, it is seen that a total sum of Rs.273230.00 in main
office was paid towards DLR payment during the year 2011-12. As per H & UD Deptt. Order No.129/2000-3605/dt.15.12.2000 read with order
no.16880/H & UD dt.18.9.99, it is instructed to disengage the DLR/NMRs engage after 19.5.97 and to regularize them with a consolidated pay
and regularize their service in respective post according to their qualification on completion of 10 years of service in respective post. Further
G.O.No.-17499/H & UD dt.10.5.98 cleared that prior sanction of Govt. is required for approval of class-III and class-IV .Also letter
no.-12204/H&UD dt.19.04.2000 denotes in case of emergency, it makes provision for temporary employment of DLR for a period not exceeding
44 days.Audit asked the local authority through objection memo whether sanction had been obtained from Finance Deptt. before engaging the
said DLRs but no reply was furnished.As such the total payment is considered as irregular and unauthorized and suggested for recovery from
the sanctioning authority basing on the above government orders.

want of connected records the entire amount of Rs.3853825.00 ( 949110+533672+2371043) is held under objection.
 
14.14 - Waste full expenditure towards engagement of consultancy  OSP-29,170,171
   During the course of checking the paid vouchers with ref. to expenditure side of  Cash Book of Div-I,BDA for the financial year 2011-12 , it is
found that a sum of  Rs.  801576.00     has been  paid to S.M.consultant ,BBSR  towards submission and approval of conceptual drawing ,
preparation of structural design ,detail estimate with specification and quantities , submission of B.O.Q. & tender documents for Multi Storied
Group Housing project over plot no.1604(Group-I) ,Paikarapur,Bhubaneswar.
 
Vr.No./Dt.            Amount            Particulars of Payments
-------------          -----------               ---------------------------------------------------------------
 81 / 204

[page 82]
 AUDIT REPORT 
09-06-2016
02/01.07.11           801576.00          Paid to S.M.consultant, BBSR for multi-storeyed building at Paikarapur
 
In support of the above fact, it is worth mention that BDA has Planning Branch, Architectural Branch and Engineering Division in which a
number of engineers, architects and planners have been engaged. Also BDA is making and approving plans of all types of buildings in large
numbers .So audit holds the view that there is no need of service of a consultancy for the regular work of the BDA employees.The local
authority was asked through objection memo as to the necessity of engagement of consultancy and produce the following records in support of
the engagement
 
          
     As per FD Memo No.42280/Codes 27/11 Dtd.26.9.11 communicated to BDA vide memo No.28232/HUD Dtd.15.10.11 The following
procedure should be maintained for engagement of consultants.
 
1. Report of Competent authority as regards services required to be performed by the consultants,
2. Approval of the competent authority,
3. Basis of awarding contract to the consultant,
4. Terms of reference,
5. Preparation of RFP (Request for Proposal),
6. Receipt of proposal,
7. Evaluation of technical and commercial bids by the committee constituted under delegation of financial rule,
8. Negotiation and award of contract,
9. Performance report of the monitoring committee,
10. Budgetary provision.
11. copies of documents as regard rendering of service by the consultant as per agreement no.Eng-1—11F2 of 2009-10 of BDA
                                                  In response to objection memo the Local authority complied that the planning branch ,architect branch and
Engineering division were busy and over burden for day today activities. In order to work out group housing scheme ,it  require conceptual
design , structural design etc. for which the projects were entrusted to private firm. The tenders have been invited by CE-cum-EM. No records
available on this office. In this regard the office has also requested office of EM for suitable reply vide our letter no.1003 dt.03.08.2015. The
copy of Agt. for the work has been provided to the Audit party.
 
                        It is construed that the expenditure towards engagement of consultancy has been made without any genuine requirement and
without approval of the competent authority.Further no proper procedure has been maintained as regards awarding contract to the
consultancy.As such the expenditure made in this regard is considered as unauthorized  expenditure and is kept under objection till production
of related documents to audit and proper compliance reported. The following officers are considered responsible for the irregulaties and lapses.
persons  responsible :
1.Asutosh Mallick ,Ex. CE-cum-EM
2.Uttam Mishra , Ex. Executive Engineer, Div-I

In support of the above payment, it is worth mention that, as per Letter No. 129/ 2000-3605 / HUD,  dt 5.12.2000 and O.M. No. 17815 (45 ) / F
Date 12.4.93 Read with Letter No. 19941 / W Date 14.09.1981   ( Works Deptt.) relating to engagement of D.L.R.s , “ Engagement of persons on
daily wages for office and field works without special written sanction of the Finance Deptt. is hereby  banned . Violation of these instructions will
result in fixation of personal responsibility on the officer engaging such persons and also on person disbursing the wages to the concerned persons
‘’
                    The local authority was asked through objection to produce the sanction order obtained from Finance Department before engagement
of DLRs.In response to objection memo the local authority replied that DLRs were engaged by BDA much before the implementation of ban on
posting of DLR by Govt. Subsequently 20 nos. Of DLRs were posted under DIV-IV, BDA on Aug 2010 vide letter no.317/EM date 22-07- 2010 of
Chief Engineer-cum- Engineer Member, BDA. Hence no violation to the Govt. Instruction has been made by DIV-IV, BDA.
                    The details of date of appointment of DLRs and sanction order obtained from Finance Department could not be made available to
audit. Hence the appointment is considered as unauthorized and the total amount of  Rs.235620.00 is suggested for recovery from the
appointing authority and drawing and dispersing officer basing on the above Govt. order.

14.18 - Official acknowledgement wanting. OSP-36
 
           On  checking the paid vouchers with ref. to Cash Book of Div-IV , BDA  for the financial year 2011-12 , it is found that a sum of Rs. 
736837.00 in shape of cheques  has been paid to Sales Tax  Officer , Circle-II, Bhubaneswar  towards  Sales Tax (VAT ),deducted from
various  work bills , the details of which is furnished below  :

  Total 736837 
In support of the above fact it is worth mention that required amounts of Sales Tax / VAT should be deposited in proper head of accounts in
Treasury by prescribed  challan form.  But here the deposits are not made in prescribed challans, rather cheques are given to STO .Hence in
support of the above said deposits of sales tax / VAT ,the local authority is asked to produce  the official acknowledgements from concerned Sales
Tax Officers    as a token of receipt of VAT amounts , through objection memo. But the Local authority failed to produce the same. In absence of
the official acknowledgement the entire amount cannot be said to be deposited in Govt. Treasury. and hence the total amount of Rs.736837.00 is
held under objection till the production of  official acknowledgement/money receipt.
 
 
 
 
 
 
14.19 - Stock entry and utilisation  wanting  OSP-36
  On checking of paid vouchers w.r.t. the expenditure side of the cash book of Div-IV of BDA for the year 2011-12, it is noticed that Rs.

electrical materials have been made from the electrical contractors in stead of from the authorized dealers/manufacturers . In support of the
above expenditure the local authority was asked through objection memo to clarify the reason of such purchase and produce the stock entry
and utilization of the materials purchased but the local authority remained silent over the mater.

During exit conference the local authority furnished his reply as follows-
 97 / 204

[page 98]
 AUDIT REPORT 
09-06-2016
The original estimate were prepared for supply and installation of electrical materials which were duly administratively approved and technically
sanctioned by the competent authority.Hence the supply portion of the work was split up and tendered for supply by the contractors/suppliers.Since
the work were urgent in nature,short tender call notice was floated for supply of materials. The installation work were also completed by the said
contractors during 2011-12.Hence no irregularities has been done and the objection raised may please be dropped.
 As regards production of stock/site account register the local authority produced the stock entry and utilization of stock amounting to
Rs.6086850.00. which was verified by audit. The stock entry of the balance amount of Rs.4302227.00. could not be made available to audit .Hence
the amount of  Rs. is held under objection till production of stock account.

14.23 - Irregular payment towards LIC Superannuation Scheme  OSP-174-175
 
      While checking the payment of LIC Superannuation Scheme wrt paid vouchers, pay acquaintance roll & file no.BESF-1/2010-11, total
amount of Rs.26685814.00 has been paid to the Chairman of the Trustee, BDA towards employees Superannuation Scheme during the year
2011-12. The above amount is inclusive of employer’s share and the amount deducted from the salary bill of the employees. The employer’s
share is equal to the amount deducted from the staff salary. It is further noticed that the employees of BDA are also availing EPF facility on
which the BDA is giving the equal share to the deduction amount of the employees. So it is noticed that the employees of BDA are availing EPF
facility as well as Superannuation Scheme for which the BDA has to pay much amount for both the facilities.Audit through its objection memo
asked the local authority to produce the Approval of the Govt.as required under section-124 of ODA Act 1982 but no reply was received.
                Further the money receipt of the LIC in support of the payment of the amount by the Trustee could not be made available to audit in
spite of issue of objection memo as such the payment of Rs26685814.00.as detailed  below is held under objection.

14.31 - Unauthorized expenditure towards engagement of consultancy ,OSP-23
 
                           During the course of checking the paid vouchers with ref. to expenditure side of  Cash Book of Div-II,BDA for the financial year
2011-12 , it is found that a sum of  Rs.39753.00  has been  paid to Creative Studio ,Bhubaneswar  towards preparation of structural design
calculation for  G + 1 storied for commercial complex at Kalinga Nagar, Bhubaneswar by taking all kinds of lead  and preparation of structural
drawing of plinth beam , column, roof beam ,slab lintel,chair and staircase for both ground floor and first floor as given below :
 
Vr.No./Dt         Amount            Particulars of Payments
-------------      -----------        ---------------------------------------------------------------
12/19.10.11   39,753.00      Paid to Creative Studio for structural design of Market Complex
                                                   at Kalinga Nagar , Zone-K-VIII,Bhubaneswar
 
 In support of the above fact, it is worth mention that BDA has Planning Branch, Architectural Branch and Engineering Division in which a
number of engineers,architects, Planner  have been engaged . Also BDA is  making and approving plans of all types of buildings in large
numbers .Audit through objection memo asked the local authority as to the necessity of engagement of consultancy for preparation of structural
design of a market complex and also the order of the competent authority to provide such work to a private farm.In addition to this, the following
records in support of the  engagement of consultancy were asked to be produce before audit.
                   a ) Tender/ quotations papers, comparative statements  in support of the ion of lowest bidders
                  b )  Concerned file
                 c )    Terms  and conditions of payment as well as work assigned . 
In response to the objection memo the local authority stated to have been produce the records to audit but no such records were made
available to audit. In view of the above the expenditure incurred towards engagement of consultancy cannot be said as genuine and considered
as unauthorized payment. The total amount of Rs.39753.00 is  considered as unauthorized payment  and suggested for recovery from the
person held responsible.

which is not true.In absence of official acknowledgement / Misc. receipt it cannot be said that the amount deposited by BDA has actually been
credited to the Govt. Treasury. Till production of money receipt the total amount of Rs.2677233.00 held under objection.

bills , the details of which is furnished below  :

 In support of the above fact it is worth mention that required amounts of Sales Tax / VAT should be  deposited in proper head of accounts in
Treasury by prescribed challan form . But here the deposits are not made in prescribed challans, rather cheques are given to STO .Hence in
support of the above said deposits of sales tax / VAT , the acknowledgements from concerned Sales Tax Officers  as a token of receipt of VAT
amounts, asked to produce to audit for check .

has been produced to audit  without producing the same to audit . The local authority produced only the file where in the letter to STO  a short sign
without any name of any one is furnished from  which the genuineness of receipt of VAT amount can not ascertained . The official money receipt
from STO or deposit chalan of VAT deposit  amount were not produced to audit for verification . Hence the said amount of Rs. 15,40,303.00 is held
under objection  till production of the required records to audit for check .

. The  amount has also been debited  from the Bank A/C of Div-III. But in support of this fact, the receipt of Misc. deposit of Rs. 3,00,000/- from

order of the competent authority  could not be made available to audit in spite of issue of objection memo .In absence of the above records ,the

produced to next audit .This reply of the local authority is quite embarrassing .Without production of related records, the expenditure can not
treated as genuine. Hence the total payment of Rs.300000.00 is considered as irregular and inadmissible to audit for which the said amount is

found that a sum of Rs. 1,01,094.00 has been booked as refund of royalty to Sri Prafulla Ku. Tena .Since royalty is being deducted in the work
bills and deposited with the Tahasildar regularly, refunding of  royalty to a contractor could not be understood. Further  the details of deduction
of royalty  in the work bills of the above contractor and whether the amount of royalty deducted had already been deposited with the Tahasildar
or kept in BDA account could not be clarified. In support of refunded  amount the related records as well as reason for such refund could not be
made available to audit in spite of objection memo.As such the refund of royalty to the contractor is considered as a loss of BDA fund .

admitted the objection for which the said amount of Rs. 101094.00 is treated as irregular payment as well as loss to BDA fund  and suggested
for recovery from the person held responsible.

14.50 - Irregular purchase of computer materialsOSP-236-237-
 While checking the paid vouchers of main finance with reference to bank book it is noticed that a total sum of Rs 1047378.00 has been paid to
different suppliers for supply of computers, printers, scanners etc the details of which are furnished below. As per Rule-2 of appendix-6 of
OGFR VOL-II read with O.M No-28122/F dt.13.7.2006, competitive quotation should be invited for purchase of articles for sums up to Rs.
50000.00 and sealed /open tenders should be called for purchase of materials exceeding rs.50000.00. the local authority was asked through
objection memo to produce the connected files along with tender/quotation documents, comparative statements and approval of competitive
authority to verify the procedure adopted towards economy, efficiency and transparency maintained in the purchase.The local authority neither
produced any records nor furnished any reply.Hence it is construed that the materials have been purchased directly without adopting any
purchase procedure which is considered as irregular and the total amount of Rs1047378.00 is held under objection.

14.53 - Loss  due to delayed payment of land premium of Prachi Enclave OSP-293-294
              
                                                            While checking the paid vouchers of main finance w.r.t. bank book and connected file no. 11/02, it is
noticed that a sum of Rs.33190605.00 has been paid to GA department vide voucher no.20/dtd4.8.11 through BD no.892694/ dtd.4.8.11
towards interest for a delay in deposit of land premium of land 19.900 acre in mouza Chandrasekharpur. Schrutiny of the file revealed that on
the request of BDA , G.A. department ,Odisha vide their order no.CA(A/L)/38-99/5828/ dtd.3.4.2000 allotted land measuring 19.900 acre to
BDA for execution  of social housing scheme. The advance position of land was made vide GA department letter no.13962/GA dtd.22.9.99. On
the said allotted land BDA lunched a plotted scheme Prachi Enclave I & II during 2000-2002. As per the terms and conditions of the allotment
order BDA should pay a premium of Rs.23880000.00 at a rate of Rs.12 Lakhs per acre and ground rate of Rs.5970.00 per annum at the rate of
Rs.300.00per acre. The above amount of premium should be deposited in one instalment within 60 days from the date of receipt of said
allotment order failing which the allotment of said land should automatically stand cancelled. After allotment of land , the Secretary BDA in his
letter no.6778 of BDA Dtd.26.12.2000 intimated to the Director of Estate and Ex-officio Joint Secretary to Govt., GA department, that as per
memo no.41476 dtd. 14.10.1987 of Housing and Urban Development Department, BDA would pay premium on the buildable area of 12.614
acre amounting to Rs.15136800.00. Further as per the above sited letter 10% of the premium i.e. 1513680.00 should be deposited before
execution of lease deed and the balance amount would be paid in three instalments after 2 years of finalisation of lease deed. In the first
instance BDA deposited Rs.1432800.00 on 15.12.2000 and requested the GA department to appove the lease deed. The balane amount of
Rs.80880 was deposited by BDA on dtd.18.8.2001. The Joint Secretary to Govt. and Exofficio Director of Estate vide his letter no.1587/CA
dtd.22.1.2002 reiterated the conditions of the allotment order and requested to pay the premium of Rs.23880000.00 with interest within 30 days
of date of receipt of his letter.The secretary BDA in responce to above letter  replied that since BDA had already deposited one tenth of the total
premium i.e. Rs.1513680.00(10% of 15136800.00 ) on buildale area,  the lease deed should be approved.In the mean while BDA did not take

OSP-297-298

Ananta Bihar core housing scheme ,Phase-III, at Pokhariput during 2000. As per terms and conditions of the allottement order G.A.
Department,BDA was to pay a premium of Rs.34895000.00 @ RS.25 lakh per acre for lease and that was to be deposited in one instalment in
treasury within 60 days of the date of receipt of the allotment order. The Secretary BDA in his letter no.1937/ dtd.28.4.99 intimated to the
Director of Estate and Ex-Officio Joint Secretary to Govt., G.A.Department that the land premium was charged on  institutional rate @ Rs.25
lakh per acre instead of residential rate of Rs.12 lakh per acre.Finaly G.A. Department revise the land premium vide his letter no.6424/
dtd.20.4.2000 to deposit Rs.16749600.00 by BDA. As per memo no.41476 dtd.14.10.87 of HUD Department, BDA would pay premium on the
buildable area and as such an amount of Rs.8144400.00 for AC 6.787 (Builadable area 48.62% of AC 13.958) would be deposited towards
premium. Accordingly BDA paid 1/10 th of the total premium i.e. RS.1674960.00. Again GA Department in his letter no.2929 dtd.18.2.2002
intimated that the balance premium of Rs.15074640.00 to be deposited within 30 days of the order received for execution of lease deed. In the
mean time BDA paid full land premium on buildable area i.e. Rs.8144400.00 by depositing balance premium of Rs.6469440.00 on 
dtd.19.1.2005. Ga Department further intimated vide his letter no.633 dtd.14.1.2010 for balance premium of Rs.8605200.00 (i.e. on gross area).
 
                                From the land file no.-L-14/02 audit observed that several correspondences were made between BDA and GA department
for execution of lease deed. But no result was come out. Finaly in the 114th Authority meeting (agenda 6/114) dtd.16.4.2010, BDA decided to
pay the premium on the gross land as termed in the allotment order of GA department. Accordingly the balance premium of Rs.8605200.00
was paid on dtd.12.3.2010 vide Bank draft no.217933 and submitted lease deed to the GA Department for execution of deed. But the Director
of Estate and Ex-Officio Joint Secretary to Govt., G.A.Department in his letter no.5134 dtd.3.6.2011 intimated BDA to deposit Rs.16924118.00
towards interest for delayed payment of premium. BDA also paid this amounton dtd.2.7.2011 vide DD no.731717. Finally the lease deed
executed on dtd.7.9.2011. in Disrtict Sub- Registrar Office, BBSR i.e. after a lapse of 12 years after the land allotted to BDA.
 
                                BDA did not give due importance to the terms and conditions of the GA Department in the allotment order and also made
un-necessary delay in taking decision on this issue.As a result of this inordinate delay in depositing of premium and execution of lease deed ,
BDA had to give RS.16924118.00 towards interest.
 
                                 The scheme Anata Bihar core housing, Phase-III developed on the said land lunched in 2000. As by this time the total
premium Rs.16749600.00 on gross land was not paid, only the premium towards the buildable area i.e. Rs.8144400.00 was included in the cost
of the scheme towards land cost.Hence BDA suffered a loss of Rs.8605200.00 (Rs.16749600.00-Rs.8144400.00).In addition to this payment of

14.61 - Fictitious purchase of broad leaf grass-OSP-115-116
                                              While checking the paid vouchers of Horticulture Division w.r.t. cash book, connected M.B and Stock Register it is
noticed that a sum of Rs 120000.00 has been paid to BBSR Regional Co-operative Marketing Society Limited towards supply of 10000 sqr feet
of broad leaf grass at Netaji Enclave Housing, Gadakana. The supplier has submitted delivery challans as follows.
 
Challan no & Date of delivery Quantity Supplied Truck no. Place of Delivery
2802/20-7-2011 1000 sqft OR-02-M-7458 Netaji Enclave Housing
2803/21-7-2011 1000 sqft OR-02-F-9472 Netaji Enclave Housing
2804/21-7-2011 1000 sqft OR-02-P-0361 Netaji Enclave Housing
2805/21-7-2011 1000 sqft OR-02-M-7488 Netaji Enclave Housing
2806/22-7-2011 1000 sqft OR-02-P-0361 Netaji Enclave Housing
2807/22-7-2011 1000 sqft OR-02-F-9474 Netaji Enclave Housing
2808/22-7-2011 1000 sqft OR-02-M-7458 Netaji Enclave Housing
2809/23-7-2011 1000 sqft OR-02-F-9472 Netaji Enclave Housing
2810/23-7-2011 1000 sqft OR-02-F-9461 Netaji Enclave Housing
2811/25-7-2011 1000 sqft OR-02-M-7458 Netaji Enclave Housing
Total 10000 sqft    
 
                              It is seen from the above challan that the Garden Assistant has signed the challan , but not furnished any certificate as regard
the quantity and quality of the materials received. As per Rule 100 of OGFR VOL-I – all stores received should be examined, counted, measured
or weighed as the case may be when delivery is taken. It should also be seen that the stores are of requisite quantity and quality to which effect a
certificate must be given by the responsible Govt. Officer on the back of the respective invoice. The Officer receiving the stores should also be
required to give a certificate that he has actually received the materials and recorded them in the appropriate Stock Register. But no such
certificate has been furnished by the Garden Asst. Who received the materials supplied.
 
                              Further it is seen from the MB no.6 that no stacking measurement has been recorded by the Garden Asst. On the spot on the
date of delivery and recorded in the MB.
                              Further it is seen that the name of the truck driver and his signature in support of the transporting the materials to the work site
is not available on the delivery challan for which it can not be ascertained that the materials are actually transported by the trucks the no. of which
are not mentioned in the body of the challans .
                              It is seen from the Stock Register Act page-77 that 10000sqft of broad leaf grass has been entered inthe Stock Register on dt
18-8-2011 i.e. after lapse of nearly one month. Giving reference to the bill no 2163 /dt 1-8-2011 of BRCMS Ltd. and  shown issue  to Sri J.
Harichandan, Garden Asst. on dt 1-8-2011 . it is amatter of stage that how the materials  received on dt. 18-8-2011 would be issued on dt
.1-8-2011. The supplier has submitted  his bill bearing the no.2163/dt 1-8-2011 after which the Stock entry has been made and shown issued to
the Garden Asst. on back date.
 129 / 204

[page 130]
 AUDIT REPORT 
09-06-2016
                              Further verification of the MB no.6 vide page no.11 revealed that no detailed measurement of plantation area was recorded by
the Garden Asst. In the MB. He has only furnished the certificate on dt.12-9-2011 as regards the utilisation of 10000sqft of broad leaf grass at
Netaji Enclave Housing Project, Gadakana giving reference to the suppliers bill no.2163/dt.1-8-2011. The certificate has been counter signed by
the Asst. Horticulture Officer BDA on dt 12-9-2011. It could notbe understood how the AHO ascertained the actually quantity of plantation without
making check measurement.
                              In view of the facts stated above audit holds the view that neither 10000sqft of broad leaf grass has been supplied by BRCMS
nor it has been utilised in Netaji Enclave Housing Project, Gadakana. The payment of Rs 120000.00 has been paid to the supplier against fictitious
supply.

14.63 - Improper utilization of broad leaf grass  OSP-107-108
                                                                                    While checking the paid vouchers of Horticulture Division w.r.t. stock register and
connected MBs ,it is noticed that a sum of Rs.1143000.00 has been paid to Bhubaneswar Regional Co-operative Marketing Society towards
purchase of 85500 sqft of broad leaf grass for plantation in different parks under BDA. The material purchased has been entered in the stock
register and issued to the Garden Assistants for plantation. Checking of the connected MBs revealed that the garden assistants have furnished
a certificate as regard the quantity of grass supplied and utilised in the concerned park which has been countersigned by the AHO. Since the
 130 / 204

[page 131]
 AUDIT REPORT 
09-06-2016
plantation of grass is made in a particular area which is a measurable item, the details of measurement showing the area of plantation should
be taken in the MBs and check measurement by the competent technical person should be made. Without the measurement and check
measurement the utilisation of the quantity of grass purchased cannot be said as genuine. Further the higher technical person cannot ascertain
the proper utilization without making check measurement.  The details of purchase ,place of utilisation and connected MBs are furnished below
In response to the objection memo the local authority did not give any reply.Hence the objection hods good .without measurement and check
measurement it can not be said that the  broad leaf grass has been utilized properly. Improper utilization resulted in loss of fund amounting to
Rs1143000.00 which is suggested for recovery from the person concerned.

14.64 - Irregular purchase of ornamental plantsOSP--98-99
                                                                                                               
                                                                While checking of paid vouchers w.r.t. the cash book a total sum of Rs.1332600.00 has been paid to
the National Agricultural cooperative federation of India Ltd. (NAFED) for supply of ornamental plants. It is seen  from file no.146
H-P-1999/2006-07 that the plants have been purchased from NAFED without floating the tender. As per O.M.No.28122/F/13.7.2006 ,sealed
/open tender should be called for purchase of materials exceeding Rs.50000.00, but the Govt. order has been violated. Instead of purchasing
the ornamental plants from the producers through open tenders, purchase has been made from a mediatory marketing society. Further there
are 165 Govt. nurseries under Horticulture Department who are producing ornamental plants as per the report of the Horticulture Department.
AS such purchase of ornamental plants could have been done from the Govt. Nurseries also. The reason of purchasing the plants directly
through a mediatory ignoring all above procedure is considered as irregular and non maintenance of economy and efficiency as envisaged in
O.M.No-28122/F Dt.13.7.2006 of Finance department,Odisha  The details of payments to NAFED are as follows.

14.65 - Irregular purchase of Planting materials,Cowdung manure and Garden soil OSP-99-101
                                                                                                                While checking of paid vouchers w.r.t. the cash book a total sum of
Rs.3813919.00 has been paid to the Bhubaneswar Regional Cooperative Marketing Society for supply of planting materials, cow dung manure
and garden soil. It is seen from file no.146 H-P-1999/2006-07 that the purchases have been made from BRCMS without floating the tender. As
per O.M.No.28122/F/13.7.2006 ,sealed /open tender should be called for purchase of materials exceeding Rs.50000.00, but the Govt. order
has been violated. Instead of purchasing the materials from the producers through open tenders, purchases have been made directly from a
mediatory marketing society. Further there are 165 Govt. nurseries under Horticulture Department who are producing planting materials  as per
the report of the Horticulture Department. AS such purchase of planting materials could have been done from the Govt. Nurseries also. Further
in respect of purchase of cow dung manure/garden soil  it could have been made from the OUAT Farmhouse or through open tender. The
reason of purchasing the plants directly through a mediatory ignoring all above procedure is considered as irregular and non-maintenance of
economy and efficiency as envisaged in O.M.No-28122/F dt.13.7.2006 of finance Department. The details of payments to BRCMS are as
follows.

14.66 - Excess refund due to wrong calculation of compounding feesOSP-240-247
 133 / 204

 AUDIT REPORT 
09-06-2016
                              The scheme called regularisation of unauthorised /Deviated construction through comunding was introduced by BDA vide
their notification no.10824 Dtd.28.12.2007. Compounding fees were fixed for various catagories of buildings and the applicant was asked to
deposit schrutiny fee and self assessed compounding fee along with the required documents.The differential amount if any should be paid
within 30 days from the date of receipt of intimation from BDA and extra amount if any should be refunded to the applicant without any intrest.
As per the above regulation the following applicants had deposited self assessed compounding fees for regularisation of their unauthorised
construction. In the mean while the above scheme was modified vide notification no.11166/ dtd.31.12.2008 in which the rate of compounding
was fixed as follows.

Further as per Para II(X) of the modified regulation the differential amount already deposited by the applicant under the ungoing scheme should be
refunded to the applicant by the authority. As such the compounding fee was calculated as per the rate prescribed in the modified scheme and the
differential amount of the following applicant was refunded.
                                                                                As per the regulation two types of rates were prescribed: 1. Construction undertaken deviating
approved plan, 2. Construction undertaken without approval of plan for various catagories of buildings. Schrutiny of the files with reference to paid
vouchers and bank book reveled that in the following cases the calculation of compounding fees was not made proporerly which resulted in excess

M.B.No.                                       :1370, Pg.-125-166
J.E.                                              :Jyoti Ranjan Sahoo,(Civil)
Exe.                                             :Sri Prafulla Ku. Jena
 
On checking of the work case bills w.r.t. the  work files, estimation, analysis of rate , MBs it is observed that a sum total of Rs. 122712.66(details
attached) has been paid in excess by allowing extra rate for loading   of excavated material during transportation of excavated material  by
mechanical means (Item no.-2) .  As the  excavation work in item no.1  includes initial  lead of 50 mtr and  lift of 1.5 mtr , thus the loading
charge   (2/3 rd of rate of loading and  unloading charge i.e. 2/3 Rs.61/cum ) of  the excavated  material has to be deducted from the rate of
transportation  of excavated material by mechanical  means   (item no.2). So there is no justification of allowing loading  charge for
transportation of excavated material as  excavation and loading  can  be done simultaneously from economic point of view.So transportation
fare=Rs.101.50 – (2/3 x Rs.61) =Rs.101.50- Rs.40.66 = Rs.60.84 .Excess paid /cum  = Rs.101.50- Rs.60.84 = Rs.40.67

Objection memo has been issued for recovery of the excess amount paid . But the local authority replied that the excavation area is beyond the
reach of transportation.The excavation material dumped within the initial lead and lift. Extra lead and lift was executed during transportation by
mechanical means. Also the transported materials were disposed with further initial lead and lift. So no excess payment has been made. Rather
than BDA has saved money not allowing further lead and lift executed during disposing the excavated material.
                                                                                                                 As there is no provision of stacking of the excavated material in the
estimate, the question of extra lead and lift during transportation does not arise. As the rate of transportation of excavated material by mechanical
means  i.e. Rs.101.50/cum includes the loading(2/3 rd) and unloading (1/3 rd) charges of Rs.61.00 ,thus the loading charge of Rs.40.66/cum has
to be deducted from the rate of transportation.Hence the excess amount paid for loading of the excavated material @40.66/cum, which comes to
Rs.122713.00 needs to be recovered from the contractor failing which the following persons will be held responsible for the monetary loss to BDA.

15.5 - Splitting up of works of Div-I, OSP-131-135
 
                While checking  the work vouchers w.r.t. connected MB, approved estimate & files, it is noticed that the following original works have
been executed by DIV-I  during 2011-12. Though the original estimates have been technically sanctioned & administratively approved by the
competent authority, after approval the original estimates have been splitted up to small quantities i.e. within the limit of Rs 50000.00 and
executed through the contractors. Besides splitting up the following irregularities are noticed in execution of the following works. 
1.       The original estimate has been splitted up to many groups within the limit of Rs 50000.00.
2.       Proper tender process as per Rule 68 of ODA Rule 1983 has not been adopted. Advertisement at least in one local news paper
circulating within the area under the jurisdiction of BDA has not been made.
3.       Order of the competent  authority has not been obtained before splitting up of the approved estimate.
4.       After approval of the original estimate the Asst. Executive Engineer has given suggestion to split up the estimate on the ground of
urgency. But the circumstances which gave rise to the urgent situation have not been clarified.
5.        The split up order has been passed by the Executive Engineer after approval of the original estimate by the Engineering Member.
6.       Only a few numbers of contractors have taken part in the tender process due to lack of wide publicity.
7.       All the splitted works in respect of single estimate have been executed by the same contractor which is not at all a time saving process.
Hence the splitting up of work is not at all beneficial and contradictory to the word “urgency” which has been used in the file for splitting up of
work.
Objection memo is issued to clarify the above irregularities  to audit failing which audit will hold view that all the irregularities are quite
intentional to provide financial benefit to the specific contractors by allotting the work at their suitable rates.
In response to the Objection memo the Local Authority replied as follows.
The splitting of different works has been done for various works as per necessity and site condition. The splitting up in each case has been
done after due approval of competent authority i.e. CE-cum-EM as per the provision of OPWD code. The various works has been taken up with
various contractors. All the works has been taken up at the estimated cost as such no extra expenditure has been made. All the splitting up has
been done after approval from CE-cum-EM. For which no wide circulation has been made and also no financial benefit to any specific
contractor has been provided. Hence the objection raised by audit may kindly be dropped.
The reply of the local authority does not touch the above points of objection.Hence the objection stands.

15.6 - Excess expenditure due  to awarding contract to L2 bidder .OSP-185-186
 
Name of the Project                       : Construction of compound wall at different patches at Kalinga Nagar, Bhubaneswar
Estimated Value                               : Rs.4471250.00
Amount put to tender                   : Rs.4466781.00
Agreement amount                         : Rs.4649919.00
Amt. as per execution                   : Rs.5082691.00
Excess Amount                                 : Rs.432772.00
Agreement No.                               : Eng-I-P-I-03  of  2011-12
Tender File                                         : Eng-I-16(T)/2011
M.B.No.                                               :1407 (Page.1-96 )
Name of Agency                             : Sri Rajesh Ranjan  Khuntia
Name of J.E.                                       : Sri Rebati Ramana Kanungo
Date of Commencement             : 22.8.11
Date of completion                      :  21.5.12
Vr. Nos., Amount, MB Nos.          : 21/8.12.11, 16/8.2.12, 49/31.3.12, 33/23.6.12
                                                      
While checking the work bill w.r.t. the connected MB and file, it is noticed that the total estimated cost of the work amounting to Rs.4466781.00
was put to tender through e-tendering system vide TCN no.BDA/EE/186/10-11 Dtd.17.2.2011. In responce to the TCN the following persons
submitted their tender papers and financial bids which were accepted after evaluation on dtd.18.3.2011.
 152 / 204

[page 153]
 AUDIT REPORT 
09-06-2016
 
Name of the Bidder                                                                        Quoted %
1.Sri Rajash Ranjan Khuntia                                        4.30% excess
2.Sri. Parikhit Barik,Eng. contractor                           9.00 less
 
            Tender of P.Barik was considered as non responsive assigning the reason that he had not submitted ESI certificate and R.Khuntia was
asked for negotiation.The negotiated rate of 4.10% excess over the estimated cost was agreed upon by both the perties.As such the total
agreement value comes to Rs.4649919.00. Further it is verified that there is deviation of 14 % excess over and above the estimated cost of
Rs.Rs.4466781.00. As such the total payment to the contractor comes to Rs.5082691.00 which is Rs.615910.00 excess over the estimated
cost of the work.
                                Scrutiny revealed that the L1 bidder Sri.P.Barik is an Engineering contractor who had tendered the work at 9.00% less than
the estimated cost which comes to Rs.4064770.00. Sri Barik had submitted an affidavit in which he mentioned that he would produce ESI
registration certificate during the time of agreement. A copy of application to the Regional Director, ESI registration office, BBSR stamped with
official acknowledgement was also produced for authenticity of applying for ESI code.(pg.no.13 of Eng-I-16(T)/2011).  But Sri.Barik was neither
given any opportunity for production of ESI certificate nor called for any negotiation. His tender was rejected arbitrarily simply on the ground that
it is non responsive bid due to non submission of ESI certificate.Had the tender of Sri Barik be considered, the work could have been executed
at Rs.4443083.00 (9% less over the executed value of Rs.4882509). As such rejection of the L1 bid simply because of non submission of ESI
certificate which could have been obtained during agreement resulted in excess expenditure of Rs.639608.00. (Rs.5082691-
Rs.4443083.00).Hence the very purpose of the tender process is defeated in this case as the BDA sustained extra financial burden.The reason
of such irregularity is asked through objection memo but the local authority  remained silent.Hence the entire amount needs to recovered from
the person concerned i.e. Sri Asutosh Mallick, CE cum EM who has given approval.                                          
                       
 
The tender was through e-procurement system. As per the clause of DTCN and as per the notes of the senior DAO the tender of Sri. P.Barik
has been considered as non responsive due to non submission of ESI document. There was no such provision in the DTCN for furnishing any
undertaking regarding any unsubmitted document.
As such the single tender of Sri R.R.Khuntia has been sent to CE-cum-EM for approval and the tender was approved by CE-cum-EM.
Since no provision in DTCN was there to give opportunity for production of ESI certificate and as in the time of tendering process the agency
did not furnish the ESI certificate the single tender was approved by the higher authority i.e. CE-cum-EM. Hence no irregularities have been
made and very purpose of tender process has been kept high as per the clauses of DTCN.
The objection raised by the audit may kindly be dropped.
In view of the compliance submitted by the local authority it can be concluded that the L2 bidder Sri R.R.Khuntia should have been asked to
execute the work as per the rate quoted by the L1 bidder Sri.P.Barick failing which re-tender should have been floated.By showing favor to Sri
R.R.Khuntia,BDA sustained a loss of Rs.639608.00 which needs recovery.
                                                                                                                                                                                                                 
Responsible Person for this paragraph

the total cost of the project has already crossed the estimated cost without executing few items. This has happened because of the increase in
quantity of work done in almost all the items from the beginning.This shows 100% deviation in the original estimate and agreement.

                                                Agr.No.                                         :Eng-I-1/4 of 2010-11                                                                                            
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   
                                                                                                                                                               
                                                Vr. No.                                         : 50/01.06.11 and 67/26.08.11
M.B.No.                                       :1404, Pg.-1-11
J.E.                                                : Sri Prafulla Ku.Sahu
Agency.                                   :     Sri Surendra pradhan.
 
          On checking of the work case bills w.r.t. the  work files, analysis of rate , MBs it is observed that a sum total of Rs.510141.00 (details
attached) has been paid in excess by allowing extra rate for loading   of excavated material during transportation of excavated material  by
mechanical means (Agt.item no13) .  As the excavation work includes initial lead of  50 mtr and  lift of 1.5 mtr , thus the loading charge   (2/3 rd
of rate of loading and  unloading charge i.e. 2/3 Rs.61/cum ) of  the   excavated material has to be deducted from the rate of transportation or
 157 / 204

[page 158]
 AUDIT REPORT 
09-06-2016
carriage  of excavated material by mechanical  means  .So there is no justification  of  allowing  loading charge  for  carriage  of excavated
material as excavation and loading  can be done simultaneously from economic point of view.
So carriage fare=Rs.102.00 – (2/3 x Rs.61) =Rs.102.00- Rs.40.66 = Rs.61.34
 
Excess paid /cum  = Rs.102.00- Rs.61.34 = Rs.40.66
 
Rate as per audit:
Earth work in ordinary soil = Rs.32.25/cum
Carriage charge                   =Rs.61.34/cum
Total                                     = Rs.93.59/cum 
Rate paid as per MB            = Rs.134.25/cum (32.25+102.00)
Excess rate paid                   = Rs.40.66       
Objection memo is issued to the local authority for clarification of the above said excess payment but in response to objection memo the local
authority replied in the exit conference as follows-
                                           In this regard the compliance furnished by the sub divisional officer for this work is enclosed  . Considering the
nature of work i.e  renovation of Pond and its depth , the rate of transportation of carriage of excavated material by mechanical means was the
lowest and most economical.The road leading to the tank is an earthen road with black cotton soil and above 250 metre from the main road
with a carriage way of 2 metre. It was therefore not possible to enter into a zone of 50 mtr lead from the tank because of the sub grade
 condition which was not sufficient enough to bear the load of the heavily loaded vehicle. Further loading of the excavated earth directly from
the tank after dewatering within a lead of 50 mtr was not possible in view of its wetness due heavy moisture content and sticky in nature. The
excavated  earth was therefore first carried to a safety place without effecting the movement of traffic , dumped to reduce the moisture content
and then transported by loading the same to the truck within the allowable limit.The rate of Rs.134.25 /cum for transportation of the earth by
mechanical means as provided in the estimate was the lowest and most economic considering all possibilities at that point of time. In view of
the above the objection raised by audit for excess payment of Rs.510140.69 towards loading of the excavated material may  kindly be
reviewed  and  dropped.    
The reply of the local authority is contradictory  to the provision made in the approved estimate due to the following reason-
(1)The earth work has been executed in  ordinary soil not in slushy soil as stated in the compliance .
(2) There is no evidence of extra lead of 250 meter beyond the initial   lead found in the estimate for loading of excavated earth.
Hence the objection stand on its own merit.

On checking the work bills with reference to work files, analysis of rates, M.B.s etc. of Div-I for the Financial year 2011-12 it is observed that
vide agr.item no.3 total quantity of late rite rock removed by chiseling is 748.44  cum (742.77 + 5.67 ) and the amount paid for this is
Rs.282162.00 @ Rs.377.00/cum. As the late rite rock or any hard rock has been chiseled, the product or out put must be late rite stones of
748.44 cum. But the MB is completely silent about the use of above quantity of Late rite stone.Hence the utilization or sale proceeds of the
above said material may be shown to audit.In the absence of production of utilization or sale proceeds the cost of total quantity of late rite stone
rough dressed @ Rs.420.00/cum (as per the Schedule of rate 2010) which comes to Rs.314345.00  is to be recovered from the contractor.
In response to objection memo the local authority replied as follows.
 
In this regard the compliance furnished by the sub divisional officer for this work is as follows .
The late rite rock after excavation by chiseling was collected in small pieces which was found not suitable for use in any work except for
disposal of the excavated materials like ordinary soil. The chisel material has no salvage value and therefore are disposed of at par with the
ordinary earth. The cost of late rite stone of Rs.420/cum is  not for the stone obtained by chiseling rather for the sized and shaped stone
obtained by cutting with much care and proper handling. In view of the above the objection raised by audit may kindly be reviewed and
dropped.
 
The reply of the local authority can be said as genuine because when a rock is chiseled, the out put must be  stone and stone dust.If it is not
so.then the rate has  been provided for chiseling of late rite rock in stead of sand stone to provide benefit to the contractor.Hence the objection
stand good.

sufficiently strengthened enough for movement of the heavy vehicle. As a result of which the excavated earth was to be carried to outside beyond
a lead of 50 mtr. and dumped. Excavating the earth from the pond and loading directly to the truck was not at all possible in this case because of
the reason that during excavation the earth was in a sticky and wet condition due to heavy moisture content in it and hence needed sometime to

causing any inconvenience to the public and dumped with a view to reduce the moisture content and then transported by loading the same to the
truck within the allowable limit.The work had been executed at the lowest possible rate considering the restrictions for movement of vehicle inside
the temple campus and also the condition of the approach road to the tank. Since there was no restriction to the public activities during execution
of work the work could not have been executed with a rate lesser than whatever provided in the estimate. In view of the above the objection raised
by audit for excess payment of Rs.52640.00  as calculated against the loading charges may  kindly be reviewed  and  dropped.

11.3.2011 and the application form for EOT has been received in the division office on 5.10.2010. The reasons shown in the application form
(Pg.200 of tender file) for extension of time are:
1. Non shifting of Transformer, 2.Non finalisation of design of road pavement, 3. Supply of grill design.The EOT has been recommended by the
Executive Engineer, Div-I and granted by the Chief Engineer.
                                                                     
   Again the Agency Sri Barik has applied EOT upto 30.8.11 ( 5 months 20 days).The reasons for EOT as mentioned in the application form 
are 1.Shifting of Electic Pole, 2. Leakage of Water Supply line and 3. Non shifting of sub station from site.He has applied for the EOT on
28.7.11 and the application form ( pg.206(A) of tender file) has been counter signed by the concerned JE on 30.7.11. The AE(c) 1 has
recommended for sanction of EOT without any levy of penalty on dtd.30.7.11.  Even though the previous date of extension completed on
11.3.11 the next EOT applied on 28.7.11 which is highly irregular.The date of receipt of application for EOT in division office has not been
mentioned.
                                                               
                                                                                As per the clause-5 of the agreement, If the contractor shall desire an extension of
time for completion of the work on the ground of his having been unavailable hindered in its execution or any other grounds he shall
apply in writing to the Vice-Chairman and/or Engineer Member of the Authority within 30 days of the date of the hindrance .Basing on
this agreement the following irregularities are noticed in allowing EOT which is not admissible to audit.
 
Irregularities noticed are as follows:
 
1.            Stipulated date of completion as per agreement                               : 11.9.10
2.            Date of receipt of application form for 1st EOT                     : 5.10.10    i.e. after the stipulated date of completion.
3.            EOT allowed in the first phase is upto                                     : 11.3.11
4.            Date for application of 2nd EOT                                                   : 28.7.11
5.            Date of receipt of application form for 2nd EOT                    : Not mentioned
6.            EOT allowed in the 2nd phase is upto                                        : 30.8.11

pavement,   
                                                                                                                                   3. Supply of grill design.                                                   
9.           The reason for EOT in 2nd phase                                                  : 1.Shifting of Electic Pole, 2. Leakage of Water Supply line and 3.
Non shifting of  sub station from site.

of work.

11. No correspondence to the Electric department regarding shifting of electric pole and transformer from the work site is found in the

sanctioned cannot be admitted in audit .
                                     As per OPWD Code 2-3-1 ,in case of delay in completion of the contract, liquidated damages (L.D.), at the rate of 1.5%
per month to be calculated per day of delay, may be levied on the value of work that remained un-finished, subject to a maximum of 10% of the
contract price.Hence 10% of the contract price i.e Rs.508891.00 (i.e. 10% of Rs.5088913.73) needs to be recovered from the contractor.
During exit conference the local authority replied as follows-
 
The E.O.T has been approved by the competent authority after recommendation by CE-cum-EM, EE and S.D.O considering the hindrances at
the site. Since K-8 area is coming under the B.D.A jurisdiction and is to be taken up By B.D.A. Hence correspondence to Electric Department
and PH Department does not arise at this stage. Hence the objection raised by audit may kindly be dropped.      
The reply of the local authority does not touch the above points of objection .Hence the para stands.

15.15 - Splitting up of works DIV-IV OSP-38,275-281
            While checking the work vouchers wrt connected MB, approved estimate & files, it is noticed that the following original works have been
executed by DIV-IV during 2011-12. Though the original estimates have been technically sanctioned & administratively approved by the
competent authority, after approval the original estimates have been splitted up to small quantities i.e. within the limit of Rs 50000.00 and
executed through the contractors. Besides splitting up the following irregularities are noticed in execution of the following works. 
1.      The original estimate has been splitted up to many groups within the limit of Rs 50000.00.
2.      Proper tender process as per Rule 68 of ODA Rule 1983 has not been adopted. Advertisement at least in one local news paper
circulating within the area under the jurisdiction of BDA has not been made.
3.      Order of the competitive authority has not been obtained before splitting up of the approved estimate.
4.      After approval of the original estimate the Asst. Executive Engineer has given suggestion to split up the estimate on the ground of
urgency. But the circumstances which gave rise to the urgent situation have not been clarified.
5.       The split up order has been passed by the Executive Engineer after approval of the original estimate by the Engineering Member.
6.      Only a ed numbers of contractors have taken part in the tender process.
7.      All the split up works in respect of single estimate have been executed by the same contractor which is not at all a time saving process.
Hence the splitting up of work is not at all beneficial and contradictory to the word “urgency” which has been used in the file for splitting up of
work.
The above irregularities may please be clarified to audit failing which audit will hold view that all the irregularities are quite intentional to provide
financial benefit to the specific contractors by allotting the work at their suitable rates.

In the exit conference the local authority replied as follows:-
Since the works are of urgent nature, the spltting has been done to complete the works in time. The splitting have been done with due approval of
the Executive Engineer who is competent to accord technical sanction as per the instruction of the Chief Engineer cum Engineering
Member,B.D.A,Bhubaneswar. The work has been done through F2 agreement with the lowest bidder during 2011-12.Hence no irregularity has
been done.

contractor which is not at all a time saving process. Hence the splitting up of work is not at all beneficial and contradictory to the word “urgency”
which has been used in the file for splitting up of work..As such the execution is completely irregular and the total amount of Rs.16211270.00 as
detailed above is held under objection.Kind attention of the administrative department is hereby drawn  toward the above irregularities.

  On checking the work bills with reference to work files, estimate, MBs etc. of Div-I for the financial year 2011-12, it is found that a total sum of
