During the course of checking  the above cited work bills of Div-II, BDA for the financial year 2011-12 , it is seen that a sum of
Rs.1,53,73,847.00 has been  paid during 2011-12  towards construction of 38 nos.of HIG Duplex Houses at Pokhariput  ,Bhubaneswar .
Further on checking the related  bills,Agreement,concerned file,Estimate, MBs , it is found that materials like G.I pipes,PVC pipes,chinaware
basins,earthen ware water closet,brass valve, H.C.I pipes,sand,K.B.bricks having crushing strength of  75kg/sq.cm. to 99kg/sq.cm.,enamel
paints,S.W.pipe,C.I.s/s pipes,UPVC pipes,cement ,M.S.rod,ceramic glazed tiles,teak wood shutters made out of well seasoned teak wood of
125mm x 32mm size ,polished granite stone,vitrified tile,stainless steel hand railings, M.S.grill, plastic emulsion paint,hardware fittings to doors
& windows,fibre glass door shutters,glazed aluminium window,sal wood chaukaths,wood shutters, marble slab, stop cock,fibre glass water
tanks etc. have been  used in construction of the house. But as per contract agreement between the BDA and the said contractor as mentioned
in agreement :
a)      Test of all the materials used in the work will be done by BDA at the cost of the contractor in any standard laboratory of Govt. in relevance
to IS code ( Agreement  Cl.11 ,page-48 )
 
b)      Test for grading,  compaction,soil test,cube test,survey & investigation as required at site will be taken up and cost thereof will be borne
by the contractor (Agreement  Cl.4-13 ,page-23 )
 
      c)Cube test for all the RCC work shall be conducted as per relevant IS specification at the cost of the contractor.
      d)All construction materials such as sand,chips,metal,cement,bricks,steel etc. are required to be tested as per relevant IS specification at
the      cost of the contractor before use in the work including periodically testing as per IS specification during execution of work
                                                ( Agreement Cl.4-12,page-23 )  
                                                                                                                                                                                
    e)Before using the materials and fixtures, prior permission/approval from the competent authority  has to be taken.The sample of materials
are to be approved before use (Agreement page-48 )
 
             Also as per Rule-5 in Appendix-VI of OPWD code, “In the case of important construction works let out on contract,articles required for
the construction of such works may be supplied by the contracting firm provided that where specifications and/or tests have been prescribed for
such articles they shall confirm to such specification and/or tests.
                In support of the above fact,it is seen that in the Estimate of the above work,provision has been made for  quality control and also
cost of the quality control of Rs. 8,14,677.00 has been provided ( Page-20 of Agreement )
                                        Hence taking into consideration of all the above fact, the Test Certificates of all  materials ,used in the work ,  from any
standard laboratory of Govt. in relevance to IS code,was asked to produce before audit for check . But the local authority did not produce any
such records to audit rather replied that
                                                   a) Test of all materials used in the work by the standard laboratory of Govt.and necessary deductions have
been effected from the contractor's bill towards testing charges .
                                                   b)  As per the Agreement condition all testing charges have been borne by the contractor .
                                                  c )  Cube testing charges  of all the RCC work have been borne by the agency .
 173 / 204

[page 174]
 AUDIT REPORT 
09-06-2016
                                                  d )  All the construction materials have been tested at the cost of the contractor .
                                                  e )  The materials have been used in the work as per the Agreement specifications .
                                                  f ) The test certificates are being produced
                                                       In support of the above reply of the local authority , no such test certificates are produced to audit for check .
Also it is seen that no deductions have been effected from the contractor's 
                                                        bill towards testing charges .As no test certificates are produced to audit , the audit is in dark to conclude the
genuineness of testing of all materials in standard laboratory of Govt
                                                       as per Agreement conditions .   Hence the above replies of the local authority is not sufficient to drop the

On  checking the above cited work bills,related Agreement,File and other records, it is found that the project has been tendered for an gross
amount of Rs.8,89,21,540.00 and the executing contractor M/S B.C.Bhuyan Construction Private Ltd. was offered the tender to do the project.
As per contract agreement bearing Agreement No.Eng-II-8-F2 of 2007-08 between M/S Bhuyan Construction Private Ltd. and BDA, hundreds
of terms and conditions were prepared for smooth execution and completion of the project. Out of all conditions, the most important condition
was about the completion  period of the project which has been described repeatedly in the Agreement bond as follows :
a)      “ We undertake to complete the work within 11(eleven) months ‘’ as described in Proforma of Submission of Tender  at Page.10 of
Agreement bond .
b)      “The entire turnkey project has to be completed and delivered within 11(eleven) calendar months from the date of issue of work order ‘’
which is given in Clause 4.7 of scope of work at page 22 of Agreement .
c)      “Time period  being the essence of the contract, the contractor shall submit from  time to time work programme along with
resource(materials,manpower,tools,plants) planning,indicating the work to be executed,lapses if any remedial measures to cover the lapses.
The period for completion of the work is 11(eleven) calendar months which is furnished at Clause-140 of “Special Condition of Agreement “
(Page-71).
                                        As per Agreement, the date of commencement of said project is : 17.10.07 . So the project work need be completed
on or before 16.9.08 . But it is found vide Vr.No. 14/30.3.12 (15th R/A) that the work was not completed till 29.3.12 and there was a violation of
Agreement regarding completion period of the project work within 11(eleven) calendar months from the date of commencement .
            Taking into consideration of “Conditions of Contract “ from the Agreement , it is noticed that as per Clause-2 at page-27, the terms and
conditions for “Compensation for Delay “ is furnished as follows  :
                        “ The time allowed for carrying out the work as entered in the tendered shall be strictly observed by the contractor and shall be
reckoned from the date of which the written order to commence the work is issued to the contractor. The work shall through the stipulated
period of the contract be carried on with all due diligence ( time being deemed to be the essence of contract on the part of the contractor) and
the contractor shall pay an compensation an amount equal to ½ % on the amount of the estimated cost if the whole work as shown by the
tender for everyday that the work remains uncommenced or unfinished after the proper dates . The work will not be considered finished until
such date as the Engineer-in-char shall certify as date of completion after necessary rectification of defects as pointed out by him or his
authorised representative as to the satisfaction of Engineer-in-charge AND further to ensure good progress during the execution of the work,
the contractor should be bound, in all cases in which the time allowed for any exceeds one month to complete one-forth of the whole of the
work before one-forth of the whole time allowed under the contract has elapsed and one half of the work before one half of the such time has
elapsed and three fourth of the work before three fourth of such time has elapsed . In the event of the contractor failing to comply with the
conditions he shall be liable to pay as compensation an amount equal two-third percent on the said estimated cost of the  whole work for
everyday that the due quantity of  works remains incomplete . Provided always that the entire amount of compensation to be paid under the
provision of this clause shall not exceed 10% on the estimated cost of the work as shown in the tender .”
Similarly as per Clause-97 described under “special condition “ at page-63, it is given that
“ If the contractor fails to complete the work by the date specified in the contract, contractor shall pay or allow BDA to deduct as sum calculated
at 0.5% of contract value per day subject to a maximum of 10% of total contract value as liquidated demages for the period during which the
said work shall so have remained in complete “
                                         In addition to the above fact it is revealed from the concerned file that no  such extension of time beyond actual date
of completion of the project, i.e., 16.9.08, had been furnished by BDA to the contractor M/S B.C.Bhuyan . The said contractor had taken random
and unauthorised E.O.T. to execute the work. Also it is noticed that in the related MBs, E.O.T. has been mentioned as detailed below, in
support of which no approval of competent authority found in the concerned file :
Vr. No./Dt          MB No.     E.O.T. as mentioned in MB
1/6.9.11             1544           E.O.T granted up to 15.8.09 (P-155)
                                                   (Date of measurement 20.7.11)
4/9.9.11             1561         E.O.T granted up to 15.8.09 (p-28)
3/3.12.11            1561        E.O.T granted up to 15.8.09
14/30.3.12          1561        E.O.T granted up to 15.8.09 (p-164)
11/23.3.12          1544         E.O.T granted up to 15.8.09 (p-196)
 
                        Hence taking into consideration of the above fact,it is concluded that the concerned contractor M/S B.C.Bhuyan had failed to
complete the work by the date specified in the contract, consumed unauthorised time to execute the said project and also violated the contract
 175 / 204

[page 176]
 AUDIT REPORT 
09-06-2016
agreement as a result of which 10% of total contract value, as liquidated demages, as calculated below, was to be deducted from the bill of the
contractor :
                    10% of total project cost executed during 2011-12
                           = 10% of Rs.1,53,73,847.00
                           = Rs. 1,53,738.00 , which is treated as loss of BDA .
 
          Through issue of audit objection memo , the local authority was asked to clarify  to audit the reasons why Rs. 1,53,738.00 was not
deducted in the R/A bill from M/S B.C.Bhuyan due to the above mentioned reasons and the  reasons for such irregularity .
                         In response to audit objection memo , the local authority replied that the 1st EOT was granted up to 15.8.2009 by the competent
authority and  then the 2nd EOT was granted by the Vice-Chairman , BDA on recommendation of inhouse committee up to 31.5.12 on dt
20.8.13 vide file no. Eng-II-Est-31/2008 .
In support of the above reply of the local authority it is worth mention that the work was commenced on 17.10.2007 and the stipulated date of
completion of the work was 16.09.2008 . But the 1st EOT was not sanctioned before 16.09.2008 and the 2nd EOT was sanctioned much time
after the completion of the project .Hence in obedience to the terms and conditions of Agreement ,it is construed that  the said contractor had
taken random and unauthorised E.O.T. to execute the work.  Hence taking into consideration of the above fact,it is concluded that the
concerned contractor M/S B.C.Bhuyan had failed to complete the work by the date specified in the contract, consumed unauthorised time to
execute the said project and also violated the contract agreement as a result of which 10% of total contract value, as liquidated demages, as
calculate above , was to be realized  from the  contractor failing of which the following persons are considered responsible for such irregularity
and the said amount will be suggested for recovery from them .

page-27, the terms and conditions for “Compensation for Delay “ is furnished as follows  :
                        “ The time allowed for carrying out the work as entered in the tendered shall be strictly observed by the contractor and shall be
reckoned from the date of which the written order to commence the work is issued to the contractor. The work shall through the stipulated
period of the contract be carried on with all due diligence ( time being deemed to be the essence of contract on the part of the contractor) and
the contractor shall pay an compensation an amount equal to ½ % on the amount of the estimated cost if the whole work as shown by the
tender for everyday that the work remains uncommenced or unfinished after the proper dates . The work will not be considered finished until
such date as the Engineer-in-char shall certify as date of completion after necessary rectification of defects as pointed out by him or his
authorised representative as to the satisfaction of Engineer-in-charge AND further to ensure good progress during the execution of the work,
the contractor should be bound, in all cases in which the time allowed for any exceeds one month to complete one-forth of the whole of the
work before one-forth of the whole time allowed under the contract has elapsed and one half of the work before one half of the such time has
elapsed and three fourth of the work before three fourth of such time has elapsed . In the event of the contractor failing to comply with the
conditions he shall be liable to pay as compensation an amount equal two-third percent on the said estimated cost of the  whole work for
everyday that the due quantity of  works remains incomplete . Provided always that the entire amount of compensation to be paid under the
provision of this clause shall not exceed 10% on the estimated cost of the work as shown in the tender .”
Similarly as per Clause-97 described under “special condition “ at page-63, it is given that
“ If the contractor fails to complete the work by the date specified in the contract, contractor shall pay or allow BDA to deduct as sum calculated
at 0.5% of contract value per day subject to a maximum of 10% of total contract value as liquidated demages for the period during which the
said work shall so have remained in complete “
                                         In addition to the above fact it is revealed from the concerned file that no  such extension of time beyond actual date
of completion of the project, i.e., 15.4.11, had been furnished by BDA to the contractor M/S Bhagabati Construction . The said contractor had
taken random and unauthorised E.O.T. to execute the work.            
                        Hence taking into consideration of the above facts , it is concluded that the concerned contractor M/S Bhagabati Constructions
had failed to complete the work by the date specified in the contract, consumed unauthorised time to execute the said project and also violated
the contract agreement as a result of which 10% of the estimated cost, as liquidated demages, as calculated below, was to be deducted from
the bill of the contractor :
                    10% of estimated cost
                           = 10% of Rs.4,78,96,871.00
                           = Rs. 4,78,968.00 , which is treated as loss of BDA .
 
         Hence the local authority was asked to clarify to  audit why Rs. 4,78,968 . 00 was not deducted in the R/A bill from M/S B.C.Bhuyan due
to the above mentioned reasons and the  reasons for such irregularity .
         In response to audit objection memo , the local authority fetched no reply and remained silent over the above objection for which audit is
in the view that the local authority admitted the objection . Hence in obedience to the terms and conditions of Agreement ,it is construed that 
the said contractor had taken random and unauthorised E.O.T. to execute the work.  Hence taking into consideration of the above fact,it is
concluded that the concerned contractor M/S Bhagabati Constructions had failed to complete the work by the date specified in the contract,
consumed unauthorised time to execute the said project and also violated the contract agreement as a result of which 10% of total contract
value, as liquidated demages, as calculate above , was to be realized  from the  contractor failing of which the following persons are considered
responsible for such irregularity and the said amount will be suggested for recovery from them .

15.21 - Non-recovery of liquidated demage from the Contractor ,OSP-268 to 272
Name of the Project                       : Construction of four laning road from Damana Chhak  to Subas Bose Enclave
E.C                                                     :  Rs. 3,66,62,850.00
Cost of project put to tender        :  Rs. 3,55,95,036 . 00
Lowest tendered value                  :  Rs. 3,39,64,659.00 ( 4.58 %  less )
Agreement No.                               : Eng.II- Est-5F2 / 2010-11
Name of  Agency                            :  M/S Bhagabati Constructions
Date of Commencement               : 18.8.2010
Stipulated date of Completion   : 17.2.2011(  06 months from the date of commencement of work, i.e.,18.8.10 )
Details of payment   :
Vr.Nos./Dt.              Amount                     Bill                       MB No ,Page no.
23/30.9.11           72,52,038.00          3rd R/A                  1571,    p-51               
9/25.11.11              9,10,052 . 00       4th R/A                   1571 , p--64
7/16.3.12             18,54,229.00         5th R/A                     1571 , p-76              
TOTAL       Rs. 1,00,16,319.00
        On  checking the above cited work bills,related Agrement , file and other records, it is found that the project has been tendered for a gross
amount of Rs.3,39,64,659.00  and the executing contractor M/S Bhagabati Constructions was offered the tender to do the project. As per
contract agreement bearing Agreement No.Eng.II- Est-5F2 / 2010-11 between BhagabatiConstruction  and BDA, hundreds of terms and
conditions were prepared for smooth execution and completion of the project. Out of all conditions, the most important conditions were about
the completion period of the project which has been described repeatedly in the Agreement bond as follows :
a)      “ We undertake to complete the work within 06 (six) months ‘’ as described in Proforma of Submission of Tender  at Page.10,para.3  of
Agreement bond .
b)      “The entire  project has to be completed and delivered within 06(six ) calendar months from the date of issue of work order ‘’ which is
given in Clause 4.7 of scope of work at page 20 of Agreement .
c)      “Time period  being the essence of the contract, the contractor shall submit from  time to time work programme along with
resource(materials,manpower,tools,plants) planning,indicating the work to be executed,lapses if any remedial measures to cover the lapses.
The period for completion of the work is 6(six ) calendar months which is furnished at Clause-140 of “Special Condition of Agreement “
(Page-56).
                                        As per Agreement, the date of commencement of said project is : 18.8.2010 . So the project work need be completed
on or before 17.2.11 . But it is found from the letter no.407/BDA,Bhubaneswar,dated 19.4.11 available in file bearing no. Eng.II-T-4/2009-10 at
page 62, and related MBs that though the period of completion of work was already over but the work have not been completed in all respects
by the stipulated date,i.e., till 17.2.11 . Also it is seen in the above said letter that only 40% of the works completed till 19.4.11and  balance 60%
left unexecuted for which M/S BhagabatiConstruction Company have neither applied the extension of time nor reported any hindrance till
19.4.11.       Hence  there was a violation of Agreement regarding completion period of the project work within 6(six) calendar months from the
date of commencement .
               In addition to the above facts ,  it is revealed from  the Agreement following conditions regarding EOT for  smooth completion of the
project  :
 179 / 204

[page 180]
 AUDIT REPORT 
09-06-2016
 
1.                      CLAUSE-5    :  EXTENTION OF TIME  (Agreement page- 25  )
                                 “ If the  contractor shall desire an extention of time for completion of the work on the ground of                    his having been
unavoidable hindered in its execution or any other grounds he shall apply in writing to the Vice-Chairman and/or Engineer Member of the
authority within 30 days of the date of the hindrance on account of which he desires such extention as aforesaid and the Vice-Chairman or
Engineer Member shall it in his opinion( which shall be final ) reasonable ground be shown thereof authorised such extention of time if any, as
may in his opinion, be necessary of proper .   The Vice-Chairman  .............”
 
2.                             SPECIAL  CONDITION           ( Agreement  page no. 49 )
 
               As described in para.94 :
   “  Requisite for extention of time to be eligible for consideration if made by the contractors in writing within 30 (thirty ) days of the happening
of the event causing the work delay . The contractor may also  if practicable indicate in such request the period for which extensions desired in
specific form approved by Engineer-in-Chief . ‘’
 
                        Hence in support of the above conditions , following irregularities  are noticed relating to execution of project work and extention
of time as detailed below :
a)      The contractor M/S Bhagabati Constructions did not complete the construction work  on or before 17.2.11,i.e., 6 months from the date of
commencement of work .As per letter no. 407/BDA,Bhubaneswar,dated 19.4.11 of Executive Engineer,Div-II, Bhagabati Constructions had not
applied for EOT till 19.4.11 ,which revealed that the contractor had not faced any hindrance during the period of execution of work but failed to
complete the work of the project in time .
 
                     
                                           Taking into consideration of “Conditions of Contract ‘’ from the Agreement , it is noticed that as per Clause-2 at
page-23, the terms and conditions for “Compensation for Delay “ is furnished as follows  :
                        “ The time allowed for carrying out the work as entered in the tendered shall be strictly observed by the contractor and shall be
reckoned from the date of which the written order to commence the work is issued to the contractor. The work shall through the stipulated
period of the contract be carried on with all due diligence ( time being deemed to be the essence of contract on the part of the contractor) and
the contractor shall pay an compensation an amount equal to ½ % on the amount of the estimated cost if the whole work as shown by the
tender for everyday that the work remains uncommenced or unfinished after the proper dates . The work will not be considered finished until
such date as the Engineer-in-char shall certify as date of completion after necessary rectification of defects as pointed out by him or his
authorised representative as to the satisfaction of Engineer-in-charge AND further to ensure good progress during the execution of the work,
the contractor should be bound, in all cases in which the time allowed for any exceeds one month to complete one-forth of the whole of the
work before one-forth of the whole time allowed under the contract has elapsed and one half of the work before one half of the such time has
elapsed and three fourth of the work before three fourth of such time has elapsed . In the event of the contractor failing to comply with the
conditions he shall be liable to pay as compensation an amount equal two-third percent on the said estimated cost of the  whole work for
everyday that the due quantity of  works remains incomplete . Provided always that the entire amount of compensation to be paid under the
provision of this clause shall not exceed 10% on the estimated cost of the work as shown in the tender .”
Similarly as per Clause-97 described under “special condition “ at page-49, it is given that
“ If the contractor fails to complete the work by the date specified in the contract, contractor shall pay or allow BDA to deduct as sum calculated
at 0.5% of contract value per day subject to a maximum of 10% of total contract value as liquidated demages for the period during which the
said work shall so have remained in complete “
                                         .            
                        Hence taking into consideration of the above facts , it is concluded that the concerned contractor M/S Bhagabati Constructions
had failed to complete the work by the date specified in the contract, consumed unauthorised time to execute the said project and also violated
the contract agreement as a result of which 10% of the estimated cost, as liquidated demages, as calculated below, was to be deducted from
the bill of the contractor :
                    10% of estimated cost
                           = 10% of Rs.3,39,64,659.00

                                                             iv)The reason for such fact is that, the rate of earth work in hard D.I.rock removed by pik axe & cowbars
is Rs.1 where as the rate of earth work in hard D.I.rock removed by chiselling is Rs.250.00  per cum as per Agreement .
                                                          v)It is to  point out that before making an estimate, the engineering staff make a survey either 
topographically or hand digging at various points to work out the underground materials of earth layers and accordingly the estimate is made .
                                                         vi)Hence it is concluded that  though earth work excavation in D.I rock by pik & crow bars is done practically
, but by showing  the work in the   name of excavation  of D.I.rock by chiselling , higher rate of work is given   in the bill to favour  the  
contractor , resulting a loss of Rs. 40,338.00  to   BDA  as calculated below :
                                Quantity of work done      =    162.00 cum
                     Rate given @ Rs. 250.00 / cum  for chiselling , i.e. , Rs. 250.00 x 162   =  Rs. 40,500.00
                     Rate of excavation of D.I rock by  pik axe     =  Rs. 1 / cum   , i.e., Rs. 162.00
                     Hence excess amount paid       =  Rs.40500.00   -   Rs. 162.00        =     Rs. 40,338.00
 
                                         Through issue of audit objection memo the local authority was  the reason for such irregular  and excess payment .
But  in response to audit objection memo , the local authority fetched no reply and remained silent over the above objection for which audit is in
the view that the local authority admitted the objection . Hence it is construed by audit that Rs 40,338.00 has been excess paid to the contractor
which needs recovery from him failing of which the amount will be suggested for recovery from the persons held responsible for such excess
and irregular payment as detailed below :

Name of the Project :  Lying morrum & sand for 60’ approach road at Pokhariput Bus Terminal
Estimated Cost           : Rs.27,42,717.00

File No.                        :    Eng-III/974(W)/2010
Name of the Agency : Sri Prafulla Ku. Jena

Name of the E.E          :Jaladhar Swain
Name of the Eng.Member : Ashutos Mallick
During the course of checking the above cited work  file along with related estimate,agreement,MBs,paid bills/vouchers and other records, it is
found that the above original work has  been technically sanctioned & administratively approved by the competent authority, but  after approval
,the original estimate has been splitted up to small quantities i.e. within the limit of Rs 50000.00 and executed through the contractors. Besides
splitting up ,the following irregularities are noticed in execution of the said work . 
1.       The proposal for splitting up the  work was placed by  Sri Biswajit Behera,JE-cum-Estimator  to Sri Jaladhar Swain,Asst.Ex.Engineer . Sri
Swain placed the file to Eng.Member,Tech.Asst. also suggested for splitting of work . Lastly the Eng. Member Sri Ashutos Mallick  approved for
splitting the work.
2.       The original estimate has been splitted up to 48 reachs within the limit of Rs 50000.00.
3.       Proper tender process as per Rule 68 of ODA Rule 1983 has not been adopted. Wide publicity within the area under the jurisdiction of
BDA has not been made.
4.       No open tender had  been made .
5.       Only one number of contactor has been taken part in the tender process
6.        All the splitted works have been awarded to same person, i.e., Sri Prafulla Ku. Jena
7.       It is revealed from the file at page no.6  that the reason for splitting of work is “ it is urgent nature of work ‘’.
8.       All the splitted works in respect of single estimate have been executed by the same contractor which is not at all a time saving process.
Hence the splitting up of work is not at all beneficial and contradictory to the word “urgency” which has been used in the file for splitting up of
work.

 It is seen from the MB that :
(i)                  During excavation by chiselling as mentioned in MB stone dust debris has been accumulated
(ii)                 12.5 %voids has been deducted frm the stone dust.
 
It is worth mention that excavation of laterite rock or any hard rock by chiselling will not produce stone dust debris  but laterite stones or hard
stones.Since the stone dust can cot be separated from the excavated earth, how the measurement was made could not be understood . If no
laterite stones or hard stone was derived from the excavation, it is construed  that excavation of laterite stone or hard rock is not done but it is
actually excavation of stoney earth & gravels mixed with stone & boulder . Hence the higher specification rate given for excavation of laterite
rock or any hard rock is not acceptable , since the rate of above specification has already been included in the rate for all kinds of soil .
                     Taking  into account these facts, it is calculated that a sum of Rs.1,44,669.00 has been excess paid to the contractor as detailed
below ,by allowing higher rate of excavation :

18.7 - 
Clarification wanting:
                                                While checking the cash book of division-III w.r.t. connected cash book it is noticed that no trasaction has been
reflected in the cash book for the period from 1.11.2011 to 30.11.2011. Since the closing balance of 31.10.11 is the same with that of 30.11.11
i.e. Rs.379550.00. But it is seen from the pass book that some cash deposits has been made in the bank account during that period. The
details of deposits is as follows.
Deposits:
Date                      Amount
3.11.11                  10930.00
25.11.11               5709.00
30.11.11               6660.00
 
Doubt arises how the amount was deposited in Bank without any reflection  made in the cash book. There might some collection made during
that period or financial transaction might have been made during that period. This may kindly be verified and clarified to audit.
